Code + script + doc references scrubbed from `internal/cli/tsdb.go` (mdemg tsdb stats), `scripts/tsdb_spot_check.sh`, `scripts/tsdb_data_review.py` + tests, `docs/api/tsdb_consumer_inventory.json` (both entries `DORMANT_TO_REMOVE` → `REMOVED`), and `scripts/verify_tsdb_consumers.py` (added `REMOVED` to the valid-disposition vocabulary + operator-hint). Live-verified: `to_regclass('ft_benchmarks')` + `to_regclass('ft_hitl_decisions')` both NULL post-migration; server restart log confirmed `schema_version=32`; verifier returns "26 declared, 26 inventoried; OK — no drift". Fully reversible via CREATE TABLE recovery from `002_ft_schema.sql` + schema-version rollback (both writers were empty at drop — no data-restore concern). ⚠️ **Rules pinned**: (1) Do NOT rewrite historical migration files — DROP-only migrations add a new file; the original CREATE stays for schema history + rollback source-of-truth. (2) When adding a `REMOVED` disposition to the inventory, extend the verifier's valid-disposition vocabulary in the same PR — otherwise the docstring/hint drifts from reality. Sprint: `docs/development/ft-dormant-cleanup-001/`.
- **Sprint JIMINY-CORPUS-002 — narrative-shaped constraint promotion gate hardening + retroactive tombstone** (2026-07-30). Q4 follow-up #2, addresses JIMINY-CEILING-INVESTIGATION-001 defect A (corpus contamination — 5 of top-12 surface volume goes to non-rules). Two-phase (mirrors JIMINY-CORPUS-001 shape): (a) forward-fix — 5 new patterns added to `DefaultConstraintPromotionRejectPatterns` blocking future promotion of Session-halt narratives, CRITICAL WORKFLOW VIOLATION logs, TESTING BLIND SPOT analyses, Foundation-document dumps, and Phase-N gap-analysis narratives; (b) retroactive tombstone (operator-authorized, backup-first) of 5 confirmed non-rule constraint nodes on `mdemg-dev` (`auto-fcb814b48e33`, `auto-015a122bcbb8`, `auto-9f5134a1a0c3`, `full-system-gap-analysis`, `llm-multi-hop-synthesis`). **Live constraint count 64 → 59.** 5 positive + 3 negative unit tests added; the negative cases pin that real constraint text mentioning junk keywords ("must run golangci-lint to catch a workflow violation", "must include a risk analysis section", "never allow a session to halt without a clean context snapshot") still promotes cleanly. Pre-tombstone state + tombstoned node IDs recorded in `docs/development/jiminy-corpus-002/backup/` for full reversibility (`is_archived=true` flag flip, nodes never leave the graph). Rollback: `SET n.is_archived=false, n.archive_reason=null, n.archived_at=null WHERE n.archive_reason='jiminy_corpus_002_purge'`. ⚠️ **Rules pinned**: (1) new reject patterns must be paired with negative unit tests using real constraint text that MENTIONS the junk keywords — anchoring with `^` where possible + specific-phrase patterns (`Foundation document for Phase`, `Phase \d+: ...analysis`) protects legitimate rules. (2) Retroactive tombstone is preferred over delete for corpus cleanup — `is_archived=true` + `archive_reason` is a reversible flag flip; nodes stay in the graph for forensic use but excluded from Lever C surfacing. Sprint: `docs/development/jiminy-corpus-002/`.
- **Sprint JIMINY-TIER1-BYPASS-001 — bypass tier1 embedding-sim for follow/ignore decisions** (2026-07-30). Q4 follow-up #4, addresses JIMINY-CEILING-INVESTIGATION-001 defect B (tier1 systematically mislabels follows as ignored — 1% follow rate on 102 real-durable-rule tier1 events per the ceiling analysis). Modifies `internal/jiminy/outcome_classifier.go::Classify` to route the [naThreshold, lowThreshold) `Ignored` verdict and the ≥highThreshold `Followed` verdict to the LLM tier2 when `JIMINY_TIER1_BYPASS_ENABLED=true`. The sub-`naThreshold` → `NotApplicable` pre-gate STAYS tier1 (a real optimization — gates truly-unrelated cases at zero LLM cost; those rows are then filtered from `constraint_outcomes` by the shipped writer gate at `service.go:1730,1762`). Config-gated default false; flipped ON in `.env` after live smoke. **Live post-flip window on mdemg-dev**: 100% llm-source constraint_outcomes rows, 0% tier1 — the bypass working as designed. 6 unit tests (using httptest LLM stub) cover every branch × flag combination. Cost analysis: ~+60 LLM calls/day (from 102/day baseline to ~162/day), well within saturation headroom; `alert_llm_health` guard held. ULTS `system_prompt_hash` pin unaffected (control-flow change in Go, not a prompt change). ⚠️ **Rule pinned**: embedding-similarity between rule text and action text is **definitionally blind to follows** — a rule "never commit to main" and an action "committed to reh3376_dev01" have low cosine sim but the action IS a follow. Any classifier that reduces the follow/ignore decision to a similarity threshold will systematically mislabel follows. The correct architectural role for tier1 embedding-sim is as a fast pre-gate for `not_applicable` (unrelated) only — where semantic distance IS the right signal. ⚠️ **Rule pinned**: when bypassing a fast-path classifier, verify the downstream path's cost + latency lift is small; live volume analysis (35% tier1 baseline, +60 calls/day estimated lift) let this ship confidently. Sprint: `docs/development/jiminy-tier1-bypass-001/`.
- **Sprint RETRIEVAL-REVERSE-LOOKUP-001 — filesystem-grep for reverse-lookup queries** (2026-07-30). Q4 follow-up #1, addresses RQA-001 cluster A ("what consumes X?" queries broken — retrieval interprets them as "find things similar to X" and returns writers/definers, zero consumers). Two-phase (mirrors RETRIEVAL-LAYER-BALANCE-001): (a) at query time, extract candidate symbols from queryText, walk the workspace filesystem (Go-native, NO shell-out) for word-boundary matches, look up matching files as MemoryNodes; (b) inject via `ApplyReverseRefQuota` post-rerank. **Query-shape gate `IsReverseLookupQuery` (whitelist of reverse-lookup verbs — consumes/uses/reads/calls/writes/references/depends/imports/queries/joins/selects/inserts/updates/deletes/affects/produces/triggers plus where/who) ensures the promoter fires ONLY for reverse-lookup-shaped queries** — critical, live-caught safety mechanism (first smoke without shape gate regressed q10 5/5→2/5). **Also live-caught**: raw grep-hit-count ranking biases toward writer/definer files (they mention the symbol many times as part of their identity); consumer files mention it 1-3 times in real reference sites. Fix: per-file HitCount CAP at 3 with deterministic tie-break (HitCount DESC, path-length ASC, lexical). **Live q11 mdemg-dev**: baseline 1/10 consumers → **3/10** (event-graph-federation.md, eventgraph-002/post.md, GuidanceEffectiveness function). **Guards**: q07 4/5 unchanged, q10 4/5 unchanged (shape gate skipped promoter on both). 8 env vars, 20 unit tests, per-request `?reverse_ref=true|false` URL override, CacheKey wired (CACHE-KEY-002 contract). Fundamentally impossible to solve via substrate-side indexing: MDEMG's node summaries are function-level abstractions that don't include the SQL/symbol strings from function bodies — the ANSWER isn't in the substrate. Filesystem-grep bridges to the authoritative external source. ⚠️ **Three rules pinned**: (1) When a retrieval class fails because the answer isn't in the substrate, no substrate-side indexing helps — the fix must reach an authoritative external source (filesystem, graph walk of ingested code, or ingest-time content extraction). (2) The reverse-ref quota promoter MUST be query-shape gated; firing on every query displaces natural top-K with irrelevant grep matches whenever the query contains identifier-shaped tokens. (3) For reverse-lookup grep ranking, CAP per-file hit count at a small constant (default 3) so files with "the symbol is my raison d'être" don't dominate files with real reference sites. Sprint: `docs/development/retrieval-reverse-lookup-001/`.
- **Sprint RETRIEVAL-LAYER-BALANCE-001 — concrete-recall + post-rerank quota promotion** (2026-07-30). Q4 follow-up #3, addresses RQA-001 cluster C (abstract L3+ emergent-concepts crowd out concrete L0/L1 rules for keyword-shaped queries). Two-phase intervention: (a) `fetchConcreteRecall` — supplementary role/layer-filtered cosine query over the concrete partition (mirrors Lever C shape); (b) `ApplyConcreteQuotaWithExtra` — post-rerank promoter that injects the concrete-recall candidates into top-K, bypassing the LLM cross-encoder entirely. **Live-caught during Tier-3 smoke**: RQA-001's framing was structurally incomplete — the concrete candidates ARE in the primary vector pool, but the LLM rerank aggressively demotes them below any topK cut for keyword queries (their scored representation is much lower than same-topic emergent-concept clusters that share query terms in their names). Post-rerank injection is the ONLY viable seam. **Live q14 A/B on mdemg-dev**: baseline 0/5 helpful (5 L3/L4 emergent-concepts), candidate top slot = `L1 constraint "[must] Always use CUIDv2 for identifiers"` (score 0.840) — the actual answer surfaces. **Guards**: q10 improved 4/5→5/5 (recursive-retrain actuator), q08 unchanged 2/5 (DRIFT-VALIDATION reframe — cluster B, not cluster C, out of scope). Both flags flipped ON in `.env` after live smoke. **Also fixed live**: `vector.similarity.cosine()` "Argument a is not a valid vector" error when a `MemoryNode.embedding` list is empty or wrong-dim → added `size(n.embedding) = size($embedding)` guard (IS NOT NULL is insufficient; Lever C's smaller partition happened to have uniform embeddings and never surfaced the class). 7 env vars (`RETRIEVAL_CONCRETE_RECALL_*` + `RETRIEVAL_CONCRETE_QUOTA_*`), 15 unit tests, per-request `?concrete=true|false` URL override (mirrors `?sparse=`), CacheKey wired (CACHE-KEY-002 contract). ⚠️ **Two rules pinned**: (1) When a retrieval intervention aims to surface a specific candidate class into top-K, first identify WHICH STAGE (recall / RRF / rerank / truncation) filters it out. Interventions at earlier stages are architecturally unable to fix problems caused by later stages — rerank's LLM scoring overrides fused scores entirely. Proven shape for class-preserving surfacing: fetch via targeted query, bypass rerank, inject post-rerank via a quota promoter. (2) RQA-001-style qualitative audits produce CORRECT problem statements but sometimes INCORRECT root-cause diagnoses. When the audit says "X is missing from the pool," verify LIVE whether the pool has X + where in the pipeline it drops out BEFORE picking an intervention shape (the `concrete_added_to_pool=0` log line was this sprint's tell). Sprint: `docs/development/retrieval-layer-balance-001/`.
- **Sprint REVIEW-SUGGESTED-GUIDANCE-CONSUME-001 — dormant column consumed** (2026-07-29). Follow-up #7 shipped from Q4 deep-dive / DORMANT-CENSUS-002 disclosed follow-up #2 (column-level dormant surface). `scripts/curate_guidance_corpus.py` now consumes the previously-dormant `review_grades.suggested_guidance` column (2/18 = 11% populated on mdemg-dev, ZERO code readers pre-sprint despite HITL-REVIEW-001's CLAUDE.md pin naming it "the highest-value retrain signal"). Extended `fetch_rows()`'s LEFT JOIN to also SELECT `suggested_guidance` + `grade_id` (filtered by `reversed=false`); new emission block writes a SYNTHETIC corpus row per qualifying suggestion (`label_source='sme_suggestion'`, `outcome='followed'`, `sme_source_grade_id`+`sme_source_row_id` provenance, `row_id='<orig>::sme_sug'` uniqueness suffix). Length-gated via `--min-suggestion-length` (default 40 chars) with the skip count reported in the manifest (`sme_suggestions_included` + `sme_suggestions_skipped_short`) so silent drops are visible. Flag on both Python + Go CLI. **Live Tier-3 on mdemg-dev**: default gate emitted the 173-char config.go three-sites SME rule as a synthetic row; raised gate (500 chars) correctly skipped it via `sme_suggestions_skipped_short=1`. **Pre-existing curator bug ALSO fixed**: `_table_exists("review_grades")` gate was too coarse — the shipped schema stores gold verdicts in `gold_dimensions` JSONB, not the scalar `gold_outcome` column the legacy code assumed → curator crashed every run since HITL-REVIEW-001 shipped. Decoupled into two capability checks (`have_grades` for the table, `have_gold_outcome` for the specific column) so the SME-suggestion path works even when the gold-outcome column is absent; added `_column_exists` helper. ⚠️ **Two rules pinned:** (1) **Capability detection at the column level, not the table level** — when a SQL branch references specific columns, check them individually; a table-exists gate is too coarse and the class error is common when a schema stores a value in JSONB instead of a scalar column. (2) **Dormant-column consumers must be length-gated** — free-text SME fields carry both genuine rules AND operator triage notes ("this was a duplicate entry"); gate on length with the skip counter reported so silent drops are visible. Sprint: `docs/development/review-suggested-guidance-consume-001/`.
- **Sprint RETRIEVAL-DIVERSITY-001 — post-rerank near-duplicate suppression** (2026-07-29). Follow-up #5 shipped from RQA-001's disclosed list. New `internal/retrieval/diversity.go` with pure `ApplyDiversityFilter(results, topK, cfg)` — exact-name-match dedup with a safety-net back-fill. Wired in `service.go` right BEFORE the topK truncation (correct architectural placement — filter picks from a larger pool than caller sees). Config: `RETRIEVAL_DIVERSITY_ENABLED` (default false), `RETRIEVAL_DIVERSITY_MAX_PER_NAME` (default 1 = strict dedup), `RETRIEVAL_DIVERSITY_MIN_OUTPUT` (default 1 = "prefer diverse coverage over completeness"). **Live Tier-3 on mdemg-dev**: q04 (RQA-001's cluster D pattern: 2× pre-bash-check + 2× sql = 4/5 duplicated slots) → 3 diverse results with flag ON (both duplicates dropped, 100% diverse). q14 pattern (emergent-concept clusters with unique-suffix names) NOT addressed by exact-name-match — deferred to name-prefix or embedding-cosine future work (RETRIEVAL-LAYER-BALANCE-001 or new sprint). **7 unit tests** pin exact-name dedup, safety-net minimum output, disabled passthrough, empty-name-always-diverse, MaxPerName=2 tunable, input-shorter-than-topK dedup, zero-value defaults. Flag flipped ON in `.env` after live smoke. ⚠️ **Architectural rule pinned**: diversity-filter design intent is "prefer diverse coverage over completeness" — default MinOutput=1 means a caller asking for topK=5 who gets 3 diverse results is BETTER OFF than one who gets 5 with duplicates; operators who need fixed output can raise MinOutput. Sprint: `docs/development/retrieval-diversity-001/`.

- **Sprint JIMINY-CLASSIFIER-CONTEXT-001 — generalized context-mismatch credit for the outcome classifier** (2026-07-29). Directly ships the #1 recommendation from JIMINY-CEILING-INVESTIGATION-001. Extends `resolveClassifySystemPrompt()` with a new `contextMismatchCreditClause` that generalizes the shipped `nonViolationCreditClause` (must_not-specific) to ANY constraint type where the rule is durable but doesn't govern the action's context: git-vs-file-write, code-vs-read-only-query, workflow-step mismatch, language mismatch, and the "constraint is a session log / phase description" surface-mismatch fallback. Routes context-mismatched verdicts to `not_applicable` (filtered from `constraint_outcomes` by the shipped writer gate at `service.go:1730,1762`) instead of `ignored` (inflates the actionable denominator). Config: `JIMINY_CONTEXT_MISMATCH_CREDIT_ENABLED` (code default false; flipped ON in `.env` after live smoke). Both flags can be enabled; clauses splice in narrower→broader order (non-violation first, context-mismatch second). ⚠️ **Default-off render is byte-identical to `classifySystemPrompt` const** — ULTS-CI-001 `system_prompt_hash` pin STAYS unchanged (no hash bump), same shape as the sibling sprint. **Live Tier-3 (direct-LLM on mdemg-dev)**: 3 fixtures — Fixture 1 (git-commit-rule + file-write) FLIPPED `ignored`→`not_applicable`; Fixture 2 (plan-mode-before-change + read-only-query) STUBBORN (LLM interpreted rule broadly, no regression); Fixture 3 (session-log "constraint" + unrelated grep) FLIPPED `ignored`→`not_applicable`. **2/3 clear flips + 1 stubborn + 0 regressions** — better than the sibling compliance-credit sprint's ratio at ship. Startup log confirms `context_mismatch_credit=true`. **5 unit tests** pin the default-off byte-identical invariant, flag-on splice (full + compact), both-flags-on ordering, config propagation. **Predicted lift**: 11%→35-50% honest follow rate per investigation evidence, recalibrated to 15-25% after the sibling sprint's actual-vs-predicted lesson (88% of NA routing landed on advisory outside the actionable denominator; followed count scales with surfacing volume, breaking the fixed-numerator prediction model). Passive re-measurement ~2026-08-05. **Follow-ups disclosed**: JIMINY-CORPUS-002 (second corpus purge), JIMINY-TIER1-BYPASS-001 (bypass tier1 for follow/ignore decision — 1.0% follow rate on real rules under tier1 is definitional embedding-similarity limitation). ⚠️ **Architectural rule pinned**: prompt extension via resolver method + default-off gate is the shipping pattern for classifier prompt changes (preserves ULTS hash pin); flags can compose additively in narrower→broader order. Sprint: `docs/development/jiminy-classifier-context-001/`.

- **Sprint DORMANT-CENSUS-002 — TSDB writer↔reader inventory + forcing function** (2026-07-29). Q4-frontier candidate #8. Second-pass DORMANT-CENSUS mirrors the `verify_route_consumers.py` shape onto the TSDB-table surface class. **Shipped**: `scripts/verify_tsdb_consumers.py` (greps `internal/tsdb/migrations/*.sql` for CREATE TABLE + diffs against `docs/api/tsdb_consumer_inventory.json`; fails on bidirectional drift + UNREVIEWED disposition); adjudicated inventory of all **26 TSDB tables** (24 IN_USE, **2 DORMANT_TO_REMOVE**: `ft_benchmarks` + `ft_hitl_decisions` — early FT schema stubs never wired to writers, superseded by `benchmark_runs`+`benchmark_results` and `review_grades` respectively); wired as merge-blocking CI step alongside the shipped DOC-CURRENCY-002 verifiers. **Investigation**: verified the 3 named deep-dive defects — (1) `mdemg_ft_production_drift` gauge still unemitted (alert reads tables directly, no historical trend), (2) `suggested_guidance` doesn't exist as a table but IS a column on `review_grades` with 2/18 rows populated + ZERO code readers (column-level dormant surface disclosed), (3) heuristic-classifier-share resolved by CLASSIFIER-CONSISTENCY-001. Also investigated 2 session-surfaced signals: empty-`constraint_code` guidance class persists (99 rows in 48h from various guidance_ids, root cause = guidance-item synthesis without preserved source-constraint code); retrieval reverse-lookup gap confirmed (already tracked by RETRIEVAL-QUALITY-AUDIT-001). **Metrics gauge inventory** attempted (146 emitted, 57 dashboard+alert references) but grep-diff has high false-positive rate (histogram base names vs `_p95` percentile derivatives) — gauge-level verifier deferred to DORMANT-CENSUS-003. **5 follow-ups disclosed**: FT-DORMANT-CLEANUP-001 (DROP the 2 dormant tables), REVIEW-SUGGESTED-GUIDANCE-CONSUME-001 (wire consumer for the column), JIMINY-CODE-BACKFILL-001 (empty-constraint_code investigation), DORMANT-CENSUS-003 (gauge-level verifier), plus the shipped RETRIEVAL-REVERSE-LOOKUP-001 continuation. ⚠️ **Architectural rule pinned**: every new TSDB table declared in migrations MUST be adjudicated in the inventory JSON in the same PR — CI fails the merge otherwise. Same pattern as DOC-CURRENCY-002 + DORMANT-CENSUS-001. Sprint: `docs/development/dormant-census-002/`.

### Fixed

- **Sprint RELEASE-HYGIENE-001 — RELEASE-PIN + GRAFANA-SHIP paired closeouts** (2026-07-28). Q4-frontier candidate #5, two shipped-feature gaps flagged in the Q3 deep-dive. **E1 (RELEASE-PIN)**: `docker-publish.yml` had semver metadata configured (`type=semver,pattern=v{{version}}`) but only triggered on `push:main / workflow_run:[Release] / schedule` — never directly on tag pushes. In 20 visible runs, only `push` and `schedule` triggers fired; the `workflow_run` chain from `release.yml` didn't fire once. Result: GHCR carried only `main` + `latest` tags; `MDEMG_VERSION=v0.10.1` pinning was unsatisfiable. Fix: added `push: tags: ['v*']` trigger directly. YAML validated (Python treats `on` as boolean True, harmless quirk; GitHub Actions parses correctly). Preserves `workflow_run` as backup path. **E2 (GRAFANA-SHIP)**: fresh Homebrew installs got blank Grafana. Compose template mounted `./deploy/docker/grafana/{provisioning,dashboards}` (cwd-RELATIVE paths); a fresh install running `mdemg init` in an arbitrary cwd has no such directory tree → mount fails or Grafana boots without provisioning. Fix: new `internal/cli/grafana_templates` package with `//go:embed all:staged` over a mirror of `deploy/docker/grafana/**` (15 files: 8 dashboards + 3 datasources + 1 alerting + 1 dashboards.yml + 2 notifiers); `Materialize(destDir)` writes to `<dest>/deploy/docker/grafana/**` matching the compose template's mount base; idempotent + operator-edit-safe (files with different content are preserved via sha256 compare, not overwritten); wired into `mdemg init` after the docker-compose.yml materialization step. ⚠️ **Because `//go:embed` can't cross package boundaries or use `..`**, the canonical files at `deploy/docker/grafana/**` are mirrored into `internal/cli/grafana_templates/staged/**` via `make sync-grafana-embed`; new CI check `make verify-grafana-embed` fails on drift so a dashboard edit missing the sync step is caught at merge, not shipped as a stale embed. **Testing**: 6 unit tests (manifest file count pin + writes-to-expected-tree + idempotency-same-content + preserves-operator-edits + empty-dest-errors + compose-mount-paths-resolve constant pin). `go build ./...`, `golangci-lint 0 issues`, `go test ./... -count=1` full green. `make verify-grafana-embed` green locally. **Deliberately NOT tested by drill (E1)**: pushing a real `v*` tag would cut a release; contract-pin (YAML validity + trigger config presence) is sufficient. Real E1 test happens on the next release. ⚠️ **Architectural rule pinned**: when a compose service mounts cwd-relative paths, the operator's cwd MUST contain those paths after `mdemg init`; add embed + materialize (`grafana_templates`-shape) rather than expecting the operator to clone the repo. Sprint: `docs/development/release-hygiene-001/`.

### Added

- **Sprint DRIFT-TRIGGER-001 — wire `ft_production_drift` into the RSIC actuator** (2026-07-28). Q4-frontier candidate #3 shipped as CAPABILITY WORK after the investigation reframed the drill (originally DRIFT-VALIDATION-001): tracing the drift-to-cycle-open chain surfaced that **the chain didn't exist as designed** — `ft_production_drift` was purely observational (fired an alert to the operator; nothing else); the only trigger for `Gate.EvaluateTrigger` was `training_data_ready` (RSIC pattern 29), a DATA-availability signal not a MODEL-quality signal. This sprint closes the loop by adding pattern 31 (`production_drift_detected`) that emits the SAME `trigger_training_pipeline` action, keyed on drift instead of data-readiness. Flows through the shipped Gate — all existing guards preserved (`FT_LOOP_ENABLED` default false, single-flight, interval, freshness). **Three additive pieces**: (E1) `DatasetProvider.ProductionDrift(ctx)` returns `ProductionDriftSummary{Delta, ActiveScore, LatestBenchScore, LatestBenchAt, HasActive, HasBench}` — SQL mirrors `alert.FtProductionDriftRule` verbatim (single source of truth for threshold semantics); `HasActive`/`HasBench` distinguish no-data from genuine zero-delta. (E2) `SelfAssessmentReport.ProductionDrift` field populated in `self_assess.go` alongside other TSDB datasets; nulled in `llm_reflector.go` sanitization (mirrors `TrainingReadiness`). (E3) Pattern 31 fires when `HasActive && HasBench && Delta > r.cfg.FtDriftMargin` (same config the alert rule uses — the two signals always agree on when drift is happening); emits `trigger_training_pipeline` insight with self-explanatory description (`"Production drift %.4f (active %.4f, latest bench %.4f) exceeds margin %.4f"`). ⚠️ **Dual-fire (pattern 29 AND 31 both emit trigger_training_pipeline on the same cycle) is harmless**: `deduplicateInsights` collapses to one, and Gate's single-flight guard blocks a second cycle regardless. **Live Tier-3 on mdemg-dev (actuator OFF, per plan)**: seeded synthetic score-regressed bench (aggregate 0.4 vs active 0.8655 → drift 0.4655 >> 0.05 margin); `/v1/self-improve/assess` returned `production_drift` populated correctly; `/v1/self-improve/cycle` produced pattern-31 insight with `pattern_id='production_drift_detected', severity='medium', recommended_action='trigger_training_pipeline', value=0.4655, threshold=0.05`; **0 new cycles opened** (Gate correctly suppressed via `FT_LOOP_ENABLED=false`); drill row cleaned up post-verify. 8 unit tests (1 summary shape + 4 reflect fires + 3 does-not-fire); full `go test ./... -count=1` green. ⚠️ **Architectural rule pinned**: drift is now a valid trigger reason, but ALL downstream Gate behavior is unchanged — this sprint added ONE new reason the trigger insight can fire, not new mutation surface. The safety envelope is identical to the shipped `training_data_ready` path. Feature doc: `docs/features/drift-triggered-retrain.md`. Sprint: `docs/development/drift-trigger-001/`.

- **Sprint CLASSIFIER-CONSISTENCY-001 — `heuristic_share_high` alert (durable observability for LLM-saturation bursts)** (2026-07-28). Q4-frontier candidate #2 shipped in reduced scope. The deep-dive framed a "25% heuristic in constraint_outcomes vs 0.03% in guidance_training_rows" as a chronic measurement-integrity issue; **investigation reframed the finding**: BOTH writers use the SAME `cr.Source` from ONE classifier call (fresh writes byte-identical), and the 25% headline was dominated by ONE burst on 2026-07-21 (451 of 473 heuristic rows in the 7d window) when 8 sprints shipped simultaneously and LLM saturation forced the classifier's heuristic fallback for ~35% of the day (baseline 0-1%). The pattern is saturation-driven and transient, not chronic; the guidance_training_rows-vs-outcomes split comes ENTIRELY from GUIDANCE-AUDIT-001's asymmetric retroactive relabeling (audit fires on training_rows only because retrain consumes that table). LLM-HEALTH-CANCELLATION-ALERT-001 fixed the underlying class; this sprint adds the durable observability that surfaces WHEN it recurs. **Ships one alert rule**: `alert.HeuristicShareRule(threshold, lookbackHours)` — fires when `count(*) FILTER (WHERE classifier_source='heuristic') / count(*)` exceeds threshold over the window on `constraint_outcomes`; distinct Service `heuristic-share` (NOSILENT-001 cooldown-key), Severity MEDIUM, ForDuration 6h. Config: `HEURISTIC_SHARE_THRESHOLD` (0.05 = 5%, ~5× baseline), `HEURISTIC_SHARE_LOOKBACK_HOURS` (24). Idle-safe COALESCE + NULLIF(count(*),0) guards divide-by-zero (TSDB-CONSUME-001 contract). Wired in serve.go; sweep tests auto-cover; 2 targeted unit tests pin defaults + custom params + SQL invariants. **Live Tier-3 (mdemg-dev)**: rule count 29 → 30 (new rule loaded); live query returns single non-NULL row `heuristic_share=0.00623` (0.62% actual, 24h breakdown 2/321 rows); threshold=0.05, gt → CLEAR (correct — steady state well below burst threshold). **Deliberately NOT shipped**: extending GUIDANCE-AUDIT to `constraint_outcomes` (option "b" in operator discussion) — marginal value (telemetry-only table; consumers are dashboards + alerts, not substrate mutators; steady-state 0-1% heuristic below noise floor). ⚠️ **Architectural rule pinned**: burst-vs-chronic distinction — a deep-dive can be right about the CLASS of failure while wrong about the TEMPO; before shipping a "chronic issue" fix, check for burst-vs-baseline via daily trend, not just aggregate-window slice. Sprint: `docs/development/classifier-consistency-001/`.

- **Sprint HITL-CURATION-002 — auto-curation layer over HITL-REVIEW-001** (2026-07-27). Candidate #1 from the Q4 frontier deep-dive. HITL-REVIEW-001 platform shipped but 7d activity = 2 grades total; 5 contradicted-drafts + 59+ guardrail rows sat unreviewed. Six sequential epics ship the friction-removal layer without removing the operator. **The load-bearing invariant**: every auto-grade row's `grader_id` starts with `auto:` (pin-tested; grep-testable) and the CLI ALWAYS POSTs with `reinforce:false` — auto-grade NEVER triggers the substrate side-effect. Only operator-confirmed grades reinforce. E1 shipped `internal/review.Autograder` (LLM-graded confidence-marker over rated rubrics) + `GET /v1/review/candidates` (bulk-fetch bypassing the human sampler bias) + `ReviewableDataset.AutogradePromptHinter` (optional dataset-specific anti-pattern language) + `mdemg review autograde` CLI + `contradicted_drafts` hint (rule-vs-log-vs-completion typology, tuned after a 2026-07-27 dry-run where the model labeled a "DATAPRUNE EXECUTED" log as `durable_rule=4`). E2 extended to `llm:guardrail.evaluate` via per-call-site hint dispatch (`llmAutogradeHints` map on `llmCallSiteDataset`); surfaced structural limitation for LLM-output correctness (model can't judge correctness without independent ground-truth; invariant + NoopSink safety net make this zero substrate risk). E3 shipped `mdemg review cadence [--out-format text|json]` — compact digest of pending items across all datasets, ready for a supervised scheduler hook. E4 shipped `alert.HITLCurationStalledRule(minPending, lookbackHours)` — fires when pending ≥ threshold AND zero **operator** grades in the window (auto-grader's own rows EXCLUDED via `grader_id NOT LIKE 'auto:%'`; else the auto-clear would suppress the stall signal). Config: `HITL_CURATION_STALL_MIN_PENDING` (5), `HITL_CURATION_STALL_LOOKBACK_HOURS` (168 = 7d); distinct Service `hitl-curation` (NOSILENT-001), Severity MEDIUM, ForDuration 24h. **Live Tier-3 (mdemg-dev):** E1 drill graded 5 contradicted-drafts (4 confident + 1 pending); E2 drill graded 3 guardrail rows; **all 6 auto-graded rows landed with `grader_id='auto:mdemg-llm-v1@dev'` and `reinforcement_applied=false`** (invariant proven live). Contradicted drafts stayed 5/5 pending (auto-grade never mutated substrate). Rule count 28 → 29; live stall-query returns 5 (exactly the pending count, threshold=5, `gt` operator → boundary-correct). All 21 unit tests green, `go test ./... -count=1` green, `golangci-lint 0 issues`. ⚠️ **Architectural rule pinned**: auto-grade rows enrich the review corpus (training-signal-quality metadata) but NEVER mutate the substrate; the reinforcement side-effect is operator-only, invariant enforced at BOTH the grader_id prefix AND the endpoint's `reinforce:false` gate. Feature doc: `docs/features/hitl-auto-curation.md`. Sprint: `docs/development/hitl-curation-002/`.

### Changed

- **Sprint NODE-DROP-CALIBRATION-001 — graph_node_drop alert calibration** (2026-07-27). Triggered by chronic false positives during the FULL-INGEST + RECLUSTER maintenance pass on 2026-07-27 (recluster tightened mdemg-dev L1 patterns 5210→4729, net −362 nodes = 0.4% of an 84k substrate → CRITICAL alert re-firing every 12 min for the rolling hour). Same defect class as ORPHAN-ALERT-001: fixed absolute threshold `>100 nodes` (0.12% of a mature substrate, 20% of a 500-node scratch space), no min-node significance floor, CRITICAL severity for what is identity-preserving cleanup. Extracted the inline `graph_node_drop` DefaultRules entry to `alert.GraphNodeDropRule(minNodes, ratioThreshold, absoluteThreshold)` mirroring `alert.OrphanRules(...)`: split into `graph_node_drop_ratio` (`GRAPH_NODE_DROP_RATIO_THRESHOLD` default 0.10 = 10%) + `graph_node_drop_count` (`GRAPH_NODE_DROP_ABSOLUTE_THRESHOLD` default 10000, ~10× the largest operator-authorized recluster delta observed live), min-node floor (`GRAPH_NODE_DROP_MIN_NODES` default 50, matches `ORPHAN_RATIO_MIN_NODES`), distinct Services (`graph-node-drop-ratio`/`-count` per NOSILENT-001), Severity **HIGH** (CRITICAL reserved for genuine data-loss emergencies). Preserves the TSDB-CONSUME-001 idle-safe COALESCE(MAX(...),0) contract; auto-covered by the `TestAllRules_NoLimitOneAntiPattern` + `TestAllRules_DistinctServicePerSeverity` sweep tests. **Live Tier-3:** simulated the new rules against real historical `metric_samples` at the exact recluster in-window moment — mdemg-dev drop 77 (0.09%) → ratio verdict SUPPRESSED (0.0009 < 0.10), absolute verdict SUPPRESSED (77 < 10000); the OLD rule would have fired CRITICAL. Min-node floor query: `global` (2 nodes) + `rune-smoke-001` (2 nodes) correctly EXCLUDED, `lnl-demo-whk` (9812) + `mdemg-dev` (84334) ELIGIBLE. ⚠️ **Architectural rule generalised (extends the ORPHAN-ALERT-001 pin):** any new per-space graph-health delta alert MUST gate on a min-node significance floor, use windowed COALESCE(MAX(...)) aggregation, distinct Service per severity, and reserve CRITICAL for actual data loss. Sprint: `docs/development/node-drop-calibration-001/`.

- **Sprint RUNE-SAFE-STRINGS-001 — repo-wide rune-safe string cutting** (2026-07-24). TSDB-WRITER-UTF8-001's disclosed follow-up: Go `s[:n]` cuts at BYTE offsets and can split a multi-byte rune, producing invalid UTF-8 that poisons TSDB flush batches (postgres SQLSTATE 22021 fails the whole batch), lands mojibake Neo4j names, and degrades LLM prompts. Three shared primitives in `internal/sanitize` (`CutRuneSafe`/`CutRuneSafeSuffix`/`TailRuneSafe`, byte-budget semantics preserved — cut backs ≤3 bytes onto a rune boundary); **34 sites fixed across 33 files**, ~12 scattered local truncate helpers now delegate to the one implementation (jiminy, guardrail, llmclient, ftloop, encoding, languages, cli). Destination classes: TSDB poison-row (endpoint-health ErrorMessage, metrics path labels, bench output tail, filer fingerprint), Neo4j names (constraint/correction/L5/reclassifier), LLM prompts (consulting classify ×2, summarize, gaps, ape reflector ×2, jiminy, ingest), API/logs/CLI (incl. `titleCase` first-rune capitalization + 6 language parsers). ⚠️ Audit lesson: the first grep pass used `head -60` and silently hid 10 sites (incl. the consulting hot-path) — audit greps must run unbounded. Live Tier-3: 193-byte CJK constraint observation on a scratch space → consolidation → Neo4j name landed at 118 bytes valid UTF-8 (120 straddled a rune; the cut backed to the boundary). Full suite + lint green, zero behavior changes on ASCII paths. Sprint: `docs/development/rune-safe-strings-001/`.

- **Sprint JIMINY-ACTIONABILITY-INVERSION-001 — diagnose why advisory guidance is followed more than actionable** (2026-07-21). Investigation-only sprint (no code changes) following DASHBOARD-TRUTH-002's suspicious finding: Should-Follow (actionable, 12.5%) is LOWER than raw Follow Rate (all types, 14.6%) — sign inversion vs the panel's premise. **E1** reproduced the inversion stably across 24h/7d/30d windows (constraint 7-10%, correction 20%, learning 12-24%, pattern 17-21%). **E2-E6** tested 5 candidate hypotheses. **Verdict**: not a bug in any single component — emergent behavior from three intentional design elements interacting: (1) constraint semantics (imperative must/must_not language) → actions that don't violate but don't demonstrate applying the rule are correctly `ignored`; (2) **Lever C over-surfacing (the dominant driver)** — `JIMINY_GUIDANCE_CONSTRAINT_BIAS_ENABLED` surfaces constraints at **22.7 events per unique ID vs 9.9 for advisory** (2.3× denominator inflation); (3) LLM classifier correctly applies stricter must vs flexible should criteria per its own prompt semantics. Hypothesis verdicts: H1 classifier asymmetry PARTIALLY CONFIRMED (correct behavior, not a bug); H2 4-band restoration MINOR CONTRIBUTOR (constraint 7.8% in sub-0.20 band vs pattern 3.8%); H3 Lever C surface bias STRONGLY CONFIRMED; H4 duplication SAME AS H3; H5 prompt bias REFUTED. **Key side-finding**: `constraint_outcomes` table has ZERO `not_applicable` rows because `service.go:1730,1762` correctly gates them out of both Neo4j and TSDB writes — the follow-rate denominator ALREADY excludes sub-0.10 not-applicable rows; the 7.8% sub-0.20 band in the table is borderline-relevant Lever-C-over-surfaced. **Fix spec (`fix_spec.md`)**: 3 fixes ranked. Fix 1 (panel wording reframe) — zero risk, closes interpretation gap; recommend ship as small doc-only sprint. Fix 2 (non-violation credit for must_not in classifier prompt) — HIGH LEVERAGE, LOW RISK; expected to route ~50% of current `ignored` constraint rows to `not_applicable` which filters out of the denominator → constraint follow rate should rise from 10% to ~20%, closing the inversion. Spec'd as follow-up sprint JIMINY-ACTIONABILITY-COMPLIANCE-CREDIT-001. Fix 3 (reduce Lever C top-K) NOT recommended — cuts coverage; Fix 2 achieves the same denominator reduction without quality tradeoff. Sprint: `docs/development/jiminy-actionability-inversion-001/`.

- **Sprint BENCH-SIDECAR-APPLY-001 — `--apply-tsdb`: benchmark persistence without the manual psql step** (2026-07-21). Closes the twice-disclosed follow-up: `--persist-tsdb` only writes a SQL sidecar, and both recent benchmark runs stumbled on the forgotten manual apply. New `persist.apply_to_tsdb` (one transaction; psycopg v3 → psycopg2 fallback, deferred soft imports; `_connect` test seam) + `run_benchmark --apply-tsdb` (sidecar still written as the audit artifact; apply failure NON-FATAL with the sidecar as recovery — the UVTS best-effort pattern). **Live smoke surfaced a real env gap**: NEITHER psycopg nor psycopg2 existed in system python or `neural/.venv` — meaning the UVTS runner's TSDB persistence had ALSO been silently skipping in this environment; `psycopg[binary]>=3.1` now pinned in `neural/pyproject.toml` [training] + installed. Live Tier-3: `tsdb applied: 2 statements` with zero manual steps; smoke rows then deleted (operator-confirmed, run_id-keyed, count-verified) restoring `ed062ea8` (0.9188) as latest. Sprint: `docs/development/bench-sidecar-apply-001/`.

- **Sprint APE-REFLECT-EVAL-REFRESH-001 — ape.reflect eval rows regenerated from post-budget-fix production; 0.623 → ~0.94** (2026-07-21). The FT dashboard's ape.reflect drop was an eval artifact: valid_clean's 20 ape.reflect rows froze pre-APE-PROMPT-BUDGET-001 prompts (~4,000 est. tokens) whose outputs clip mid-array under llama-server's 8,192-token per-slot KV bound (`finish_reason=length` ×5; `json_valid` honestly 0) — the April baseline "passed" only because mlx_lm.server had unbounded KV. New reproducible `scripts/refresh_ape_reflect_eval_rows.py` replaced ONLY those 20 rows with chronological-spread samples from 5,487 clean post-2026-06-14 production rows (~1,400 est. tokens max; response must parse as a JSON array; other 220 rows byte-identical). Leak-audit CLEAN 0/240 vs the original 9 sources. **[AMD-2] governance**: both manifests re-pinned (`f215a34a…` → `79fd8e24…`) with a full amendment entry; backup retained. **Live verification (run `ed062ea8`)**: ape.reflect `finish=stop` ×5, `json_valid=1.0` ×5, task mean ~0.94; **aggregate 0.8544 → 0.9188** — the honest current-model number the dashboard now shows. ⚠️ Rule pinned: when production behavior is intentionally changed (prompt budgets, serving limits), audit pinned evals for rows that embody the OLD behavior — a production-frozen eval measures its era's serving constraints, not just the model. Sprint: `docs/development/ape-reflect-eval-refresh-001/`.

- **Sprint J17-TIER-GATE-001 — T1 promotion keyed on measured comprehension; first live T1 messaging** (2026-07-21). The operator's "we never get to T1" investigation landed on the root: T1 required session trust > 0.75, but trust is a COMPLIANCE EMA (live 0.23 under the ~72%-ignored mix) while the risk dense encoding actually guards against is INCOMPREHENSION — separately measured (48h avg 0.732) with its own T1→T2 demotion gate. New `J17_TIER_GATE_MODE` (`trust` default = byte-identical legacy, pin-tested | `comprehension`): in comprehension mode `selectTier` promotes coded items to T1 when `ProtocolMetricsCollector` overall AvgComprehension ≥ `J17_TIER_COMPREHENSION_HIGH` (0.6 — the existing ineffective-threshold anchor) with ≥ `J17_TIER_COMPREHENSION_MIN_SAMPLES` (20) in-process events; cold-start/nil-provider falls back to legacy trust logic; the demotion gate stays active in both modes. **Live Tier-3: the first T1 messaging the protocol has ever produced** — 6 pipe-coded lines (`C:!|use-cuidv2-by-default`, ~5-15 tokens each vs ~40-80-token T2) in the live `encoded_augmentation` after 3 real feedback rounds lifted in-process comprehension to 1.0. Discovered + documented: in-process comprehension is composition-sensitive (15 tier1-band-ignored samples read 0.000 regardless of count) so **T1 re-locks after each restart until ~an hour of real traffic accumulates** (in-memory-reset, by-design cold-start); steady-state comprehension (~0.61-0.73) sits near the 0.6 floor — flap tuning via the config knob, demotion gate as safety. `.env` runs `comprehension` mode (operator-authorized); code default stays `trust`. Sprint: `docs/development/j17-tier-gate-001/`.

- **Dashboard fixes from operator round-2 J17/RSIC/FT review** (2026-07-21). Three panel fixes from the operator's second metrics-of-concern list. **(1) Trigger Rejection Rate** (`mdemg-rsic.json`): title now says "(per min)" + description answers the operator's unit question — the SQL computes rejections/minute; 5-10/min is the RSIC-STORM-001 by-design rate (~40 rejections per admitted cycle). **(2) Avg Time Between Compactions** (`mdemg-j17.json`): was permanently "no value" from TWO defects — `$__timeFilter` on a rare-event stream (6 compact events over 26 days; any window <~2 events → NULL) AND `COUNT(*)` over repeated gauge-flush rows instead of DISTINCT event timestamps (82 rows, 6 real events — interval massively understated when it did compute). New SQL: DISTINCT values over the last 10 lifetime events, no window filter. Thresholds were also INVERTED (rewarded frequent compaction); now longer-is-better (red <6h, yellow 6-24h, green >1d). **(3) Entropy Health → "LLM Output Quality (HITL-graded)"** (`mdemg-ft-training.json`): the panel read `llm_interactions.quality` — NULL on all 91,468 rows ever (the "post-hoc annotation job" it expects was never built; TSDB-CONSUME-001 writerless-panel class). Repointed at HITL `review_grades.gold_score` for `llm:*` datasets (live: 2 grades, avg 0.750) with honest "No HITL grades in window" empty state. rsic JSON also picked up compact→expanded formatting normalization (json.dump; no structural change beyond the panel edit).

- **J17 protocol gauges — no-data-as-zero gate on restart (fix during J17 dashboard investigation)** (2026-07-21). The J17 protocol stats live IN MEMORY and reset on every server restart; both gauge publishers (`internal/ape/live_collectors.go::publishProtocolGauges` + `self_assess.go::publishProtocolMetrics`) published `compression_ratio=0.0` and `tier fractions 0/0/0` from zero-event windows — the DASHBOARD-TRUTH-002 rule-#3 no-data-as-zero class. On restart-heavy days (5+ deploys today) this diluted the "Average Compression" panel's windowed average and latched lastNotNull panels to zero. New shared `j17EventGaugesMeaningful` predicate (one chokepoint, both publishers) skips the event-derived Sets (tier fractions, compression, comprehension, tokens/guidance, per-tier comprehension) when `TotalEvents==0` — the gauges stay absent from the flush so panels hold the last real value; honest-at-zero gauges (events_total, outcome counts, replay, ticket-restore per DH-004, code-coverage) still publish. Sentinel-pinned (`TestPublishProtocolMetrics_ZeroEvents_SkipsEventDerivedGauges`). Live Tier-3: post-restart flush contains `events_total=0` but NO fresh compression/tier rows.

- **Sprint JIMINY-ACTIONABILITY-COMPLIANCE-CREDIT-001 — non-violation credit for must_not in classifier prompt** (2026-07-21). Fix 2 from the JIMINY-ACTIONABILITY-INVERSION-001 fix_spec.md. Extends the tier-2 LLM classifier prompt (`internal/jiminy/outcome_classifier.go::classifySystemPrompt` + compact variant) with a "non-violation credit for must_not" clause that routes unrelated-context `ignored` verdicts to `not_applicable`, which the shipped writer gate (`service.go:1730,1762`) already filters from `constraint_outcomes`. **Predicted lift**: constraint follow rate 10% → ~20% by shrinking the actionable denominator (see `baseline.md` — 46 followed / 451 total → 46 / ~201 if ~50% of the 503 ignored verdicts shift to not_applicable). Ships behind default-off config gate **`JIMINY_NONVIOLATION_CREDIT_ENABLED`** per the "flag flipped only after live smoke" contract; operator runs the 3-day A/B recipe (`ab_recipe.md`) before flipping in `.env`. ⚠️ **Design choice: default-off render is byte-identical to the historical `classifySystemPrompt` const** — this preserves the ULTS-CI-001 `system_prompt_hash` pin without a hash bump; the flag flips runtime-rendered prompt only. Pin-tested via `TestResolveClassifySystemPrompt_DefaultOff_ByteIdentical`. **Live Tier-3 A/B on mdemg-dev** (direct-LLM against llama-server, temperature 0.0): clear-cut cases (unrelated/following/violating) both prompts agree (correct); **discriminative borderline case** (`must_not amend pushed commits` × `edit-a-config-file`, sim 0.55) shows OLD=`ignored` vs NEW=`not_applicable` — same reasoning text ("didn't touch the mechanism"), different classification — proving the clause does exactly what fix_spec.md predicted. **Also bundled Fix 1** (panel wording reframe): Grafana `mdemg-jiminy.json` panel renamed "Should-Follow Follow Rate" → "Actionable Compliance Rate"; description explains the metric is EXPECTED to read lower than raw follow rate under Lever C architecture; thresholds recal green ≥0.5 (was ≥0.9) / yellow ≥0.15 (was ≥0.5) matching the honest 10-25% baseline. Alert rule `guidance_should_follow_rate_low` title updated; `GUIDANCE_SHOULD_FOLLOW_RATE_FLOOR` default recal 0.5 → **0.05** (the `lt`-alert floor must sit BELOW the ~0.10-0.14 by-design steady state — live 168h rate 0.1137 — so only genuine collapse fires; an interim 0.15 shipped briefly in E6 with inverted reasoning and was corrected same-day in a follow-up fix-commit; panel bands now red <0.05 / yellow 0.05-0.18 / green ≥0.18 post-flip target zone). Sprint: `docs/development/jiminy-actionability-compliance-credit-001/`.

- **Sprint FT-BENCH-REFRESH-001 — fresh benchmark on GGUF endpoint + staleness detection** (2026-07-21). Closes the DASHBOARD-TRUTH-002 stale-data finding: `benchmark_runs` had a single row from 2026-04-24 (88 days old) against the pre-Phase-13.5 MLX model, so the FT Latest Run panel showed 87-day-old scores as if current. **E1** preflight verified llama-server + valid_clean.jsonl + TSDB writable. **E2** fresh benchmark against current GGUF endpoint (`mdemg-llm-v1.Q5_K_M.gguf` via llama-server :8102) on `valid_clean.jsonl`: run_id `jc81749c2d95d7be029d2c614`, aggregate **0.8544** vs 88-day-old baseline 0.8338 (**+0.021** — GGUF outperforms the old MLX baseline, no Phase 13.5 regression). ⚠️ **Learning captured**: `--persist-tsdb` writes a SQL sidecar file, does NOT directly INSERT — must `docker exec -i mdemg-timescaledb-1 psql < sidecar.sql` to land rows. Also `--rows-per-spec 5 --n-runs 1` (~45 calls, ~12min) is the right scope for a refresh; the plan's `--rows-per-spec 0 --n-runs 5` (~1200 calls, hours) was too heavy. **E3** dashboard cross-check: Latest Run panel now displays fresh row. **E4** staleness alert rule `ft_benchmark_stale` (`internal/alert/rules.go::FTBenchmarkStalenessRule`) — reads `benchmark_runs` directly (not scheduled_job_events; benchmark writer is a standalone Python CLI); idle-safe SQL `COALESCE(EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM (now() - MAX(completed_at))) / 86400.0, 999.0)` (TSDB-CONSUME-001), distinct Service `ft-benchmark` (NOSILENT-001), Severity HIGH, 1h eval interval. Config: `FT_BENCH_STALENESS_DAYS` (default 7); ≤0 falls back to default with WARN. Wired unconditionally in serve.go — the only signal operators get when they've forgotten to refresh. **E5** new "Latest Benchmark Age" stat panel (id 25) on `mdemg-ft-training`, above the Per-Task Pass Rate panel — reads the same SQL as the alert rule, colors mirror the alert (green <3d, yellow 3-7d, red >7d). **E6 live Tier-3**: fresh row age 0.0004d << 7d default (OK); force-stale via `launchctl setenv FT_BENCH_STALENESS_DAYS 0` + kickstart → rule would fire on next eval; restored. `TestFTBenchmarkStalenessRule` pins the contract; `TestAllRules_NoLimitOneAntiPattern` + `TestAllRules_DistinctServicePerSeverity` sweep-tests auto-cover the new rule. Follow-up disclosed: FT-RECURSIVE-002 should either wire the sidecar apply into an automated psql step OR the runner should gain a direct-INSERT mode. Sprint: `docs/development/ft-bench-refresh-001/`.

- **Sprint DASHBOARD-TRUTH-002 — second wave of dashboard/measurement artifact fixes** (2026-07-20). Follow-up to DASHBOARD-TRUTH-001 (2026-07-03). An operator-flagged sweep across 5 dashboards → 5-agent read-only triage classified 14 concerns; **9 measurement/dashboard artifacts** closed here (3 REAL-LOW already in flight per JIMINY-CORPUS arc; 1 designed-behavior needing a companion panel only; 1 stale-data → FT-BENCH-REFRESH-001; 1 suspicious-inversion → JIMINY-ACTIONABILITY-INVERSION-001; 1 bonus /metrics 404 → PROMETHEUS-SCRAPE-INVESTIGATION-001). **E1** RSIC Retrieval dimension: `scoreRetrieval` had a wrong-anchor enum table (`saturated=0.7 < warm=0.9`) that penalised graph MATURITY as if degradation; fixed to `saturated=0.9`. **E2** RSIC Edge dimension: hardcoded 0.5 entropy floor fired forever on any mature Hebbian graph (live 40404 edges → p=0.047 → entropy=0.27 → permanent −0.2 penalty). Now config-tunable via new `RSIC_EDGE_ENTROPY_FLOOR` (default 0.2, calibrated below observed healthy; 0=disable). Triage misdiagnosed mechanism (claimed structural fixed-weight edges; actually CO_ACTIVATED_WITH evidence-count distribution) — conclusion stands, mechanism corrected in code comment. **E3** RSIC Protocol dimension: two fixes — (a) DH-004 "no data = neutral" gate applied to `restoreScore` when `TicketRestoreTotal=0`; (b) `J17_COMPRESSION_TARGET_RATIO` default 3.0→2.0 (30d live p95 is 2.0; old target was defensively-excellent but unreachable). **E4** Retrieval Pipeline Health panel: `gauge`/`lastNotNull` on a 3-row UNION silently showed only rerank (77%); converted to `bargauge` horizontal → all 3 stages visible. **E5** New Cycle Admission Ratio companion stat panel (`cycles/(cycles+rejections)`) — normalises the "scary" Trigger Rejection Rate (RSIC-STORM-001 designs aggressive rejection; the raw rate is on-spec). **E6** J17 Min/Max Trust panels: SQL now gates on `trust_session_count>=2` (via CTE) → renders "N/A" when N=1 instead of the misleading `min==max==avg==23.30%` distributional stat over a set of one. **E7** Jiminy Constraint Effectiveness: All-Time (0.25) vs Selected (0.14) diverged by ~0.10 because they read different sources (Neo4j cumulative with pre-2026-06-11 heuristic half-credit inflation vs TSDB windowed) AND different thresholds (HAVING>=5 vs 2). Unified: both read `constraint_outcomes` with `classifier_source IN ('llm','tier1','explicit')` filter for parity. **E8** FT hidden.reclassify=0.5: **stale-data artifact** (2026-04-24 pre-Phase-13.5 benchmark row where the then-spec declared both `json_valid` + vestigial `classification_accuracy`; spec later cleaned). Live writer path grep-verified already correct via `spec.reward_functions` filter; ships regression pin `test_compute_reward_output_keys_are_exactly_spec` that fails on any future widening. The stale row will be corrected by FT-BENCH-REFRESH-001. **E9** LLM endpoint state = "No data": panel read the transition-log table (zero rows during steady-state UP for 15+ days); rewrote to source `mdemg_mlx_health_state` continuous gauge from `metric_samples`. **E10** Recent watchdog events = "No data": dropped `$__timeFilter` (rare-event stream, newest event ~15d ago); always shows last 30 events regardless of range. **Live Tier-3 (mdemg-dev):** direct API self-assess returns `LearningPhase=saturated / EdgeCount=125289 / EdgeWeightEntropy=0.266`; gauges post-restart show retrieval 0.700→**0.900** (E1) + edge 0.800→**1.000** (E2) + compression sub-score 0.278→**0.555** with new target 2.0 (E3); protocol dimension will lift ~+0.15 once J17 stats rebuild post-restart (currently transient at 0.338 with comprehension/coverage still at zero). All 8 dashboards pass JSON validation; grafanapin + ape + config + python reward-keys pin tests all green; full `go test ./...` clean. New arch rules pinned: (1) RSIC dimension scoring must read real signals not enum-lookup maturity; (2) Shannon entropy over edge weights needs a config-tunable floor; (3) any success-rate metric with a no-data case must gate to neutral 1.0 when denominator is 0 (DH-004 pattern generalises); (4) multi-row gauge panels must NOT use `lastNotNull` — use bargauge or split to stat panels; (5) aggregate stats (min/max) over a live set must gate visibility on set-size floor; (6) writers must not persist reward-vector keys the spec doesn't declare; (7) panels reading "state" must use the continuous gauge NOT the transition-log. Sprint: `docs/development/dashboard-truth-002/`.

- **Sprint GRAFANA-PANEL-FILTER-001 — filter caller-cancellation noise from Grafana LLM error-rate panels** (2026-07-20). Closes the disclosed follow-up from LLM-HEALTH-INVESTIGATION-001 E3. That sprint added the `NOT LIKE 'caller_canceled:%'` filter to the ALERT-rule signal (`internal/tsdb/dataset_builder.go::LLMPerformance`) but not the Grafana panels — so `mdemg-llm-routing.json` "LLM error rate % by task_name" still showed `retrieval.rerank_cross` at 10.84% raw rate (all 27 rows caller-cancellations; 0 real errors 24h). **E1** grep-audit swept every panel in `deploy/docker/grafana/dashboards/*.json`: exactly 1 must-fix (`mdemg-llm-routing.json:80`); 7 safe `llm_interactions` panels doing non-error math; 0 ambiguous. **E2** panel edit applies `AND error NOT LIKE 'caller_canceled:%'` to both `CASE` predicates + inline SQL contract comment referencing `dataset_builder.go::LLMPerformance` as source-of-truth. **E3** new `internal/grafanapin/dashboards_test.go` — Go pin test walks every dashboard JSON (164 panel targets scanned), matches every panel target reading `llm_interactions` AND doing `error` aggregate math, fails if any lacks the required filter; whitelist mechanism `-- GRAFANA-PANEL-FILTER-001: intentionally unfiltered` for forensic/debug panels; contract-liveness assertion fails if ZERO panels match (regex regression). Sanity-verified: pin test FAILS on unfiltered pre-fix SQL, PASSES on fixed tree. **E4 live Tier-3 (mdemg-dev):** SQL cross-check shows honest_error_pct=0.00% vs raw=10.84% (27 caller_canceled rows filtered, 0 real errors hidden); `mdemg-grafana-1` container restarted, `/etc/grafana/dashboards/mdemg-llm-routing.json` contains the filter + 2× contract markers; `/api/health` database:ok. Follow-up disclosed: when the recorder adds a new noise-class prefix, extend `requiredExclusions[]` in the pin test AND `dataset_builder.go` in the same PR — the two are the enforcement pair. Sprint: `docs/development/grafana-panel-filter-001/`.

- **Sprint CONTRADICTED-BRIDGE-APPLIED-NODE-ID-001 — contradicted-draft rows now capture the applied MemoryNode's node_id** (2026-07-20). JIMINY-CONTRADICTED-BRIDGE-001's approve path stored only `applied_obs_id`, but `obs_id` ≠ `node_id` (a MemoryNode's `node_id` is a distinct CUIDv2). Downstream code that wanted to walk the *node* (e.g. verify L1 promotion, join to graph telemetry) had to re-query Neo4j to translate `obs_id → node_id`. **E1** additive V0031 migration adds `applied_node_id TEXT` to `contradicted_correction_drafts`; `ContradictedDraftRow`, `Flush`, `FetchPendingBySpace`, `FetchByID`, and `MarkApproved(id, obsID, nodeID)` all carry the new column. **E2** the HITL sink Apply captures `resp.NodeID` alongside `resp.ObsID` and threads both into `MarkApproved`; `contradictedDraftItem` Meta surfaces `applied_node_id`. **E3** extracted `contradictedDraftsWriterIface` (subset of `*tsdb.ContradictedDraftsWriter`); new Tier-1 test `TestContradictedDraftsSink_Apply_ApprovePassesBothIdentifiers` uses a capturing mock to pin both IDs flow through. **E4 live Tier-3:** V0031 auto-applied on server restart (schema 30→31); real contradicted verdict → new pending draft → `/v1/review/grade` approve → draft row now shows `applied_obs_id=cdo0jzbxh8rce8vprycz13n4` AND `applied_node_id=d6pesuwmby8tmmmzdmr5k47e`; both IDs resolve to the same MemoryNode in Neo4j (confirming they really do differ). Historical rows (pre-E1 approvals) keep `applied_node_id=NULL` — retrievable via the existing `obs_id → node_id` join; backfill deferred as low-value cleanup. Sprint: `docs/development/contradicted-bridge-applied-node-id-001/`.

- **Sprint NEURAL-RERANK-PRECHECK-001 — provider-aware rerank pre-check budget** (2026-07-20). LLM-HEALTH-INVESTIGATION-001 E2's pre-check ran BEFORE the `RerankProvider` switch, so a single knob `RerankMinBudgetMs=12000` (calibrated for LLM p99=11.7s) gated ALL providers — including neural (typical p95 ~1s). For `RERANK_PROVIDER=neural`, a caller with 5s remaining would silently over-skip a viable ~1s neural call. **E1** added `NeuralRerankMinBudgetMs` (env `NEURAL_RERANK_MIN_BUDGET_MS`, default 1500 = neural timeout 1000ms + 500ms margin, floor 500); pre-check dispatches on provider (`"neural"` → NeuralRerankMinBudgetMs; openai/ollama/jina/default → RerankMinBudgetMs, byte-identical). WARN log gains `provider` field. **Live Tier-3 (mdemg-dev, neural sidecar UP):** default openai unchanged (rerank_ms=2588); `RERANK_PROVIDER=neural` completed in **rerank_ms=122** (neural sidecar is 20× faster than LLM path on this workload); force-skip verified with `provider=neural` in the WARN log. Follow-up disclosed: neural 20× faster measurement → data-decided default-flip candidate pending UVTS A/B on quality parity. Sprint: `docs/development/neural-rerank-precheck-001/`.

- **Sprint LLM-HEALTH-INVESTIGATION-001 — rsic-alert_llm_health noise from caller-cancellation** (2026-07-20). The recurring `rsic-alert_llm_health` spike (6-12 fires/hr) was 100% caused by `retrieval.rerank_cross` cancellations — not LLM health. Investigation: 9/9 errors were `context canceled` on the llama-server POST; rerank p95=11.1s vs RETRIEVE_TIMEOUT_MS=20s meant that when earlier pipeline stages consumed 10-14s, rerank started with insufficient budget → guaranteed cancellation. **E1** recorder tags caller-cancellation with `caller_canceled:` prefix (raw text preserved after); real errors unchanged. **E2** rerank pre-check reads the CALLER's `ctx.Deadline()` before dispatching; skips (fail-open, pre-rerank RRF results) when remaining < `RerankMinBudgetMs` (default 12000 = p99+300ms margin) — no wasted llama-server slot, no misleading `caller_canceled` row. Deadline-absent callers bypass. **E3** RSIC alert-rule filter: `LLMPerformance` + `computeLLMQuality` SQL count `error != '' AND error NOT LIKE 'caller_canceled:%'`; same filter on `MAX(time)` for the SUPERVISOR-002 recency gate. **Live Tier-3 (mdemg-dev):** first post-restart rerank cancellation correctly tagged `caller_canceled:`; alert-rule SQL returns `real_errors=0` (1 all_errors filtered); pre-check skip fires deterministically under forced override (RerankMinBudgetMs=60000); 30-min window on default budget → 0 new `alert_llm_health` fires. Follow-ups: raising `RETRIEVE_TIMEOUT_MS` 20→30s (data-decided, only if skip-rate becomes non-negligible); optional skip-rate gauge for early-warning. Sprint: `docs/development/llm-health-investigation-001/`.

- **Sprint JIMINY-ROLETYPE-ADAPTER-001 — retrieval-sourced guidance was mis-typed as `learning`** (2026-07-17). Closes the disclosed follow-up from JIMINY-CORPUS-001. Every retrieval-sourced item was defaulting to `GuidanceLearning` because `RetrieveForJiminy` dropped `role_type` — the chain was empty at every seam: `models.RetrieveResult` had no `RoleType`/`ObsType` fields, `retrieval.Candidate` had none, the `vectorRecall` Cypher didn't `RETURN` them, both scorers had nothing to copy, and the adapter's mapping loop copied 5 fields. **E1** wires `RoleType`/`ObsType` end-to-end (models, Candidate, FusedCandidate, BM25Result, jiminy.RetrievalResult, both scorers, adapter, BM25 Cypher `RETURN`, RRF-merge non-empty preference). **E2** widens `classifyRetrievalItem` to prefer `RoleType == "constraint"|"correction"` before the Layer≥2 concept short-circuit and the `ObsType` switch (default fallback preserved for empty). **E3** extends the classifier truth table by 6 rows + adds a mapping pin test. **Live Tier-3:** `/v1/memory/retrieve` returns `role_type='constraint'` on the L1 UxTS constraint node; `/v1/jiminy/latest` surfaces 4/5 items typed `constraint` (all 5 would have been `learning` pre-fix); `constraint_outcomes` gains 3 `guidance_type='constraint'` rows with matched `constraint_code`. **Surprise fix caught during smoke:** the reasoning-module rerank (`internal/retrieval/reasoning.go`) rebuilds `RetrieveResult` from a proto response — extended the existing `originalByID` restore hook to preserve `RoleType`/`ObsType` (separate fix-commit per the Phase 11.6.2 precedent). Sprint: `docs/development/jiminy-roletype-adapter-001/`.

- **Sprint DASHBOARD-TRUTH-001 — make the RSIC / J17 / Jiminy dashboards honest** (2026-07-03). An operator flagged 8 alarming metrics; three Fable-5 read-only agents reproduced each live and classified ARTIFACT vs REAL-LOW — 6/8 were measurement artifacts (the LIMIT-1 / COALESCE-to-0 / stale-gauge / wrong-anchor / structurally-red-alert class). **E1:** RSIC Cycle Success Rate read 0% while RSIC is at 100% (71/71) — per-`time_bucket` ratio + `COALESCE(…,0)` + `lastNotNull` latched a started-only bucket to 0; fixed to a single windowed aggregate (+ a sibling Retrieval-Pipeline-Health gauge). **E2:** the J17 NLI bias alert was permanently red by construction (NLI *comprehension* vs a *compliance* heuristic; ~80% ignored → bias ≫ 0.15 forever) — now excludes `ignored` samples, gates on a min-sample floor `J17_NLI_CALIBRATION_MIN_SAMPLES` (50), and adds real NLI-call observability (`mdemg_j17_nli_requests_total`/`_latency_ms`; the old `mdemg_j17_sidecar_requests` counts only the dormant tier-prediction client). Hardened at the source (`Report()`) so both gauge emitters + `rsic_adapters` + the drift insight share one chokepoint. Live: 0/0, held 65+ min. **E3:** the Protocol dimension's compression sub-score was anchored to a hardcoded "5.0× = perfect" while real achievable is ~1.8–3× — extracted to `J17_COMPRESSION_TARGET_RATIO` (default 3.0, calibrated above the 30d p95=2.0); disclosed Protocol 64.5%→68.7%. **E4:** J17 trust-store hydration stamped `LastUpdate=now()`, so the 168h TTL never expired stale sessions and the flush overwrote the persisted timestamp — 116 sessions looked active (115 last-fed >168h ago; `CleanupExpired` also had zero callers). Fixed provenance preservation + wired cleanup + a gauge significance floor `J17_TRUST_MIN_FEEDBACK_COUNT` (5). Live: session count 116→1. **E5:** the Jiminy Outcome pie/trends stacked lifetime-cumulative multi-credit gauges (0.73 vs honest 0.16); replaced with windowed `constraint_outcomes` counts, and Should-Follow now excludes `not_applicable` (0.084→0.142, panel + alert rule). The REAL-LOW guidance-quality gap (follow rate ~16%) is deferred to Sprint 2 (JIMINY-CORPUS). Sprint: `docs/development/dashboard-truth-001/`.

### Added

- **Sprint JIMINY-STRUCTURED-CORRECTION-001 — propagate Incorrect/Correct/Context to L1 correction nodes** (2026-07-20). `POST /v1/conversation/correct` previously discarded the structured triple during the free-text join; downstream synthesis had to regex-parse the joined string. Now: **E1** persists `structured_data.correction = {incorrect, correct, context}` on the L0 obs (additive; preserves constraint-detector keys); extracted `buildCorrectionObserveRequest` helper for testability; retired the dead `metadata_*` param flatten in `createObservationNode` (verified live: those params were never referenced by the CREATE cypher — meta_keys=[] on a fresh L0 obs; documented in code + here). **E2** `CreateCorrectionNodes` parses the L0 structured_data and sets `correction_incorrect`/`correction_correct`/`correction_context` as top-level properties on the new L1 correction node; absent-safe for old-format L0. **E3** new `mdemg corrections rehydrate-structured --space-id <id> [--dry-run] [--batch-size 100]` backfill CLI walks L0 corrections missing structured, parses the joined content via a template regex, merges into structured_data (preserves other keys), and propagates to any linked L1 via `IMPLEMENTS_CORRECTION`. Idempotent + malformed-existing-defensive + WARN-skips unparseable. **E4** `GuidanceItem` gains three optional structured fields; Lever C fetch reads them from L1; `buildGuidancePrompt` renders correction items as "Do <correct> — not <incorrect>" when the pair is present, falls back to raw Content otherwise; Lever B `directiveSynthesisInstruction` preserves both sides of the contrast. **Live Tier-3 on mdemg-dev:** L0 (`wgrs82t8fvpunspgg12pbnqk`) + L1 (`igtz7vhy844cngs49xxv2bhw`) carry all three structured fields; backfill parsed 1/33 (the bridge-authored obs) + WARN-skipped 32 hand-authored free-form corrections (expected — they were captured via /v1/conversation/observe, never had the template shape); Lever B narrative preserved both contrast sides ("You must fix every discovered test failure" + "Previously, there was a tendency to label failures as pre-existing"). No schema migration; additive properties on the schemaless graph label. Sprint: `docs/development/jiminy-structured-correction-001/`.

- **Sprint JIMINY-CONTRADICTED-BRIDGE-001 — contradicted-outcome → correction draft bridge** (2026-07-20). Closes the pipeline's last unblocked autonomous-learning slot. Jiminy's `contradicted` verdict — the highest-signal lesson (the classifier says "the agent's action directly opposes the guidance intent" with BOTH sides in hand) — sat unused for 23 lifetime rows because nothing captured the lesson. **E1:** V0030 hypertable `contradicted_correction_drafts` + buffered async writer (`internal/tsdb/contradicted_drafts_writer.go`) with primitives-only `RecordDraft` (no jiminy import cycle), point reads + sync status transitions all flush-then-query for read-your-writes. **E2:** bridge hook in `RecordOutcome` on `OutcomeContradicted`; template-based Incorrect/Correct render; dedup via in-process LRU on `(guidance_id, action_hash)` where `action_hash=sha256[:8]` over normalized action (whitespace+case tolerant). **E3:** HITL dataset `contradicted_drafts` with new `ContradictedDraftsRubric` (0-4 `durable_rule` + `phrasing_quality`); sink derives verdict from `durable_rule` (≥3 approve → calls `conversation.Service.Correct` → real L0 correction obs; ≤1 dismiss; 2 defer); reverse resets draft to pending, L0 obs stays. **E4:** unit + adapter tests (bridge helper LRU + hash + template; sink Preview/Apply/verdict truth table; mock CorrectService captures Incorrect/Correct fields). **E5 live Tier-3 on `mdemg-dev`:** real contradicted verdict → draft persisted → HITL surfaced → `/v1/review/grade` approve → real L0 obs `po2zahas8mh10ahwe0iimmoz` → next consolidation → real L1 correction node `ymehdkihmj2yiu7t3bywsgxc` (count 32→33). Full flow works end-to-end with no mocks. **E6:** `JIMINY_CONTRADICTED_BRIDGE_ENABLED` default false→**true** (live-verified unmasked on fresh boot); `REVIEW_CONTRADICTED_DATASET_ENABLED` default true. HITL gate remains the substrate-mutation guard. Rollback tombstone-only. Sprint: `docs/development/jiminy-contradicted-bridge-001/`.

- **Sprint JIMINY-CORRECTION-PRODUCER-001 — L1 correction-role node producer** (2026-07-20). Closes the final disclosed follow-up from JIMINY-ROLETYPE-ADAPTER-001. Before this sprint: **zero `role_type='correction'` MemoryNodes existed in any space**, while 32 L0 `obs_type='correction'` observations sat in `mdemg-dev` unpromoted (real durable rules — `max_completion_tokens` for gpt-5.x, sprint plans belong in `docs/development/<sprint-line>/`, do not parallelize epics, never `mdemg db start`). `CreateConstraintNodes` had promoted tag-gated observations to `role_type='constraint'` for a long time; there was **no sibling producer for corrections**. **E1+E2:** `internal/hidden/correction_nodes.go::CreateCorrectionNodes` mirrors the shipped constraint producer, keyed by `obs_type='correction'` (semantic label, 1:1 with the obs — no type-grouping) with idempotency via the `IMPLEMENTS_CORRECTION` guard + identity `MATCH (c {role_type:'correction', name:$name})`; embedding + surprise-signal confidence propagate. `internal/hidden/correction_gate.go::CorrectionPromotionGate` mirror of `ConstraintPromotionGate` — no obs_type deny-set needed (the predicate already gates on `obs_type='correction'`); backstop is min-content-length + config-driven regex deny-set reusing the JIMINY-CORPUS-001 junk-class defaults. New config: `CORRECTION_PROMOTION_ENABLED` (default true), `CORRECTION_PROMOTION_MIN_CONTENT_LEN` (default 20), `CORRECTION_PROMOTION_REJECT_PATTERNS` (JSON regex array). **E3:** registered as `correctionStep` at consolidation phase 20 alongside `constraintStep`; 24 gate subtests (junk rejection + too-short + genuine-live-corrections pins) all green. **Live Tier-3:** consolidation created **32 L1 correction nodes (100% of candidates, 0 rejections)** + 32 `IMPLEMENTS_CORRECTION` edges (avg confidence 0.679); `/v1/memory/retrieve` returns `role_type='correction'` on the "max_completion_tokens gpt-5" query as result [0]; Jiminy `/latest` surfaces `type='correction'` (1/8 items); `constraint_outcomes` gains its **first ever `guidance_type='correction'` row for mdemg-dev** (`followed`, tier1). Rollback tombstone-only via `is_archived=true`. Sprint: `docs/development/jiminy-correction-producer-001/`.

- **Sprint RETRIEVAL-TYPED-EDGES-002 (Phase 2) — grow the semantic edges + flip typed-edge retrieval default-on** (2026-07-03). Phase 1 wired the scorer but the A/B was +0.0000 because the semantic edges barely existed. Phase 2 grew them and re-measured cleanly. **Epic 1 — `dynamic_edges` vector-index rewrite:** replaced the O(n²) Cartesian cross-join in `CreateDynamicEdges` with a per-node top-K query via the `memNodeEmbedding` vector index (O(n·logn)); lowered `DYNAMIC_EDGE_MIN_LAYER` default 3→1 (a fresh corpus has ~no L3+ concepts — semantic edges must reach the abundant L1/L2 layers); the degree cap now counts only the dynamic semantic-edge types (not the dense structural membership edges); removed the obsolete CONSOLIDATE-PERF-001 circuit-breaker (`DYNAMIC_EDGES_MAX_NODES`); fixed a CUIDv2 violation (`randomUUID()`→Go-minted). New config: `DYNAMIC_EDGE_{MIN_LAYER,TOPK,SIM_THRESHOLD,OVERSAMPLE}`. **Live: 4,415 typed semantic edges on lnl-demo-whk (from ~1) in 11.5s** (vs the old 29-min cross-join). **Epic 2 — clean A/B (quiet system, warmed):** flag OFF 0.4120 vs ON (graph w=0.15) **0.4130 → +0.0010 mean, +0.009 correct-file, 0 regressions, 5 improvements, no latency cost** (flag-on 4.2s = flag-off). The prior 16q +0.007 was small-sample noise; the prior "flag-on 8.65s" was a Neo4j-load confound, both corrected. **Epic 3 — amplification is a dead end:** raising the graph column weight 0.15→0.25 regressed (0.4130→0.4090; computed_value −0.016) — the Phase-13 finding reconfirmed (amplifying the graph column crowds out embedding/BM25). **Decision: `RETRIEVAL_GRAPH_TYPED_EDGES_ENABLED` default false→true** — fulfils the operator directive (typed edges now influence retrieval), zero downside, keeps semantic edges wired in as they grow. Feature doc: `docs/features/typed-semantic-edges.md`. Sprint: `docs/development/retrieval-typed-edges-002/` (`ab_verdict_full.json`).

- **Fix (retrieval robustness, surfaced during the Phase-2 A/B): cache-poisoning + unbounded-retrieve** (2026-07-03, merged PR #496). Root-caused a live retrieval incident. **(1) Cache poisoning:** `queryCache.Put` cached empty/context-canceled responses with no guard, so a transient-0 (from load) was served as a fast 8ms "0 results" for the whole TTL. Fix: `len(resp.Results) > 0 && ctx.Err() == nil`. **(2) Unbounded retrieve:** `handleRetrieve` used the raw request ctx, bounded only by `HTTPWriteTimeout`=600s, so the per-column timeout was `0.8×600=480s` — under Neo4j load the column queries hung for tens of seconds to minutes, starving the hooks and holding connections that compounded contention. Fix: `RETRIEVE_TIMEOUT_MS` (20s) deadline → fail-fast (and not-cached) instead of hang. **Root cause (capacity follow-up):** retrieval and consolidation contend for Neo4j — retrieval degrades *during* a consolidation window (proven: identical queries return results at neo4j CPU 1, fail at CPU 127). The fixes make it degrade gracefully/self-heal; reducing the contention is a separate item.

- **Sprint RETRIEVAL-TYPED-EDGES-001 (Sprint B) — typed semantic edges in RRF retrieval; Phase 1 shipped default-off, A/B says Phase 2 is the lever** (2026-07-01). Operator directive: typed semantic edges (ANALOGOUS_TO/BRIDGES/etc.) should influence retrieval. Built the scorer mechanism and **measured** it. **Epic 1:** the 7 typed semantic-edge attention weights (were hardcoded in `ComputeEdgeAttention`) are now `EDGE_ATTENTION_{ANALOGOUS_TO,BRIDGES,COMPOSES_WITH,CONTRASTS_WITH,INFLUENCES,DEFINES_SYMBOL,THEME_OF}` (defaults = prior literals). **Epic 2:** behind `RETRIEVAL_GRAPH_TYPED_EDGES_ENABLED` (default-off), the RRF graph column (`column_graph.go`) spreads via `SpreadingActivationWithAttention` (all typed edges, config-weighted) instead of the CO_ACTIVATED_WITH-only basic spreading; the flag + weights join `scorerVersion()` (cache namespace); the 8 keys are exposed in the `/ui/` config tab. **Epic 3 (the A/B):** ingested `whk-wms` (8,973 nodes) into `lnl-demo-whk` and ran the UVTS quick A/B — **flag off vs on: +0.0000, byte-identical across all 16 questions** (flag effect confirmed real via `retrieval_audit` `tge=off`/`tge=on` scorer versions, not a wiring bug). **Why zero (the finding):** (1) the semantic edges barely exist — their only producer `dynamic_edges` creates ~none (lnl-demo-whk: 0 ANALOGOUS_TO/0 BRIDGES vs 44k ASSOCIATED_WITH; mdemg-dev: 258/14 across 355k edges); (2) even structural-edge spreading in the weight-0.15 graph column doesn't change the RRF top-5 (embedding 0.50 + BM25 0.20 dominate; it re-ranks the same seeds without surfacing new candidates). **Conclusion:** Phase 1 is necessary but not sufficient — measure-first prevented flipping a no-op default-on. Achieving the directive needs **Phase 2**: grow the semantic edges (the `dynamic_edges` vector-index rewrite) + make the typed-edge signal expand the candidate pool (not just re-rank). New config: 7 `EDGE_ATTENTION_*` weights + `RETRIEVAL_GRAPH_TYPED_EDGES_ENABLED`. Also fixed a CI test (`config_llm_cascade_test.go`) for the local-first default. Feature doc: `docs/features/typed-semantic-edges.md`. Sprint: `docs/development/retrieval-typed-edges-001/` (`ab_verdict_quick.json`).

- **Fix CONFIG-LOCAL-DEFAULTS-001 — LLM defaults route to local, not OpenAI cloud** (2026-06-30). Operator-found via the UI config tab (`jiminy.evaluate_llm_model = gpt-4.1`). Ground truth (`llm_interactions`): all 1,495 calls/24h use `mdemg-llm-v1` via the local llama-server — no OpenAI-cloud usage; this deployment is correct (env forces local). But the DEFAULTS were a latent fresh-install footgun: `EffectiveLLMEndpoint()` fell back to `OpenAIEndpoint` (api.openai.com) when `LLM_ENDPOINT` was unset, and `LLM_MODEL` defaulted to `gpt-5.4-mini` — a no-env install would silently hit OpenAI's cloud, contradicting the Phase-13.5 always-local architecture. Local-first defaults: `LLM_ENDPOINT` ''→'http://127.0.0.1:8102/v1'; `LLM_MODEL` 'gpt-5.4-mini'→'mdemg-llm-v1'; UI `defaultValues` gpt-4.1→mdemg-llm-v1. OpenAI stays available only via explicit `OPENAI_*` config (the teacher/embedding path is unaffected). Also killed a stray `./bin/mdemg serve` process that was holding `:9999` with a stale binary (why the config tab wasn't updating).

- **Sprint CONSOLIDATE-PERF-001 (Sprint A) — consolidation per-phase instrumentation + the dynamic_edges bottleneck** (2026-06-30). The real performance work the now-truthful `neo4j-cpu` alert (ALERT-TRUTH-001) pointed at. **Measure-first paid off immediately:** the new `mdemg_consolidation_phase_duration_seconds{space_id,phase}` instrumentation (shared `metrics.RecordConsolidationPhase`, wired into BOTH the service-level `RunConsolidation` watchdog driver and the `handleConsolidate` HTTP path, + per-pipeline-step `step:<name>` timing) found the ~38-min cost on the first cycle — and it wasn't the inferred ForwardPass. **`post_clustering` ran ~29 min and hit the 30-min timeout; the per-step breakdown pinned it to `dynamic_edges`** — `CreateDynamicEdges`' find-pairs query is a Cartesian product over all L≥minLayer nodes (`MATCH (a),(b) WHERE a.layer>=3 AND b.layer>=3`), which at scale (live: **8,705 L3+ nodes → ~75.8M pairs**) dominated the cycle for a paltry yield (272 ANALOGOUS_TO/BRIDGES edges total, `LIMIT 50`/run). **Fix (data-decided):** a node-count circuit-breaker — `CreateDynamicEdges` counts L≥minLayer nodes (via the existing `memorynode_layer_idx`) and SKIPS the O(n²) cross-join loudly above `DYNAMIC_EDGES_MAX_NODES` (default 2000); small graphs keep full behavior, large graphs skip until the Sprint-B vector-index rewrite. Also raised `CONSOLIDATE_TIMEOUT_MS` default 30→60 min (the manual path aborted mid-cycle at 30). The planned cheap win `findSimilarConcept` was **dropped** (measured 1.6s, negligible). **Live Tier-3: `dynamic_edges` 29 min → 34 ms; `post_clustering` ~1,760,000 ms → 7,674 ms (~230×); the watchdog cycle (the 2-3/day Neo4j-CPU driver) 38 min → ~47 s; identity preserved (L1=5130, L3=4634 stable); CPU calm (165 < the 500 alert).** The 19-alert Neo4j-CPU class is eliminated at the source. New config: `DYNAMIC_EDGES_MAX_NODES` (2000), `CONSOLIDATE_TIMEOUT_MS` default 60 min. Sprint-B targets (now visible): `summaries_llm` 120s (manual-path LLM), forward/backward ~27s (incremental rewrite), the `dynamic_edges` vector-index rewrite. Feature doc: `docs/features/consolidation-performance.md`. Sprint: `docs/development/consolidate-perf-001/`.

- **Sprint ALERT-TRUTH-001 — alert & dashboard correctness** (2026-06-30). A Grafana review found 50 pending alerts while the substrate was healthy (RSIC 0.73–0.79, 0 breakers, ~0% real LLM error rate) — the channel was dominated by **miscalibrated rules and dashboard bugs firing on normal operation**. This is a correctness pass (no behavior-contract change) that makes the alerts and panels tell the truth. **(1) Alert-rule SQL hygiene:** the last 4 rules still on the `ORDER BY time DESC LIMIT 1` anti-pattern (`neo4j_high_cpu`, `neo4j_high_memory`, `low_cache_hit_ratio`, `jiminy_follow_rate_drop`) rewritten to windowed `AVG` + `COALESCE` so each returns one non-NULL row on an idle window (TSDB-CONSUME-001 contract; pin test `TestAllRules_NoLimitOneAntiPattern` gathers every rule group). **(2) Neo4j CPU calibration:** the fixed `80` was "% of ONE core" — on multi-core hardware normal consolidation runs 200–837%, so it fired on any real graph work (the 19-alert driver). Extracted to a config-driven `Neo4jCPURule(NEO4J_CPU_ALERT_THRESHOLD_PERCENT`, default **500**) over a 5-min windowed AVG; 500 is calibrated above the worst normal-operation 5-min windowed AVG (live 24h max 471; 0 windows >500).
